\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{\frac{p^{2}L\Xi}{\delta\lambda_{\min}\big(\sum_{\tau=L}^{T-1}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\big)}}$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.67776pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}+\|{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\Delta{\bm{G}}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:T}]}\|_{\ell_{2}}\bigg)\geq 1-\delta,

\displaystyle\implies\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\|{\hat{{\bm{G}}}-{\bm{G}}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{\frac{p^{2}L\Xi}{\delta\lambda_{\min}\big(\sum_{\tau=L}^{T-1}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\big)}}$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.67776pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}+\frac{\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{B}}}\|\|{{\bm{C}}}\|\frac{\phi({\bm{A}},\rho)\rho^{L}}{1-\rho}\sqrt{T-L}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}\big(\sum_{\tau=L}^{T-1}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\big)}}\bigg)\geq 1-\delta.

(6.13)

This completes the proof.

6.1.2
Proof of Lemma
3.2

Proof

Before we begin the proof, let
\tilde{{\bm{U}}}=\tilde{{\bm{Q}}}\tilde{{\bm{\Sigma}}}\tilde{{\bm{R}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}
be the singular value decomposition, where
\tilde{{\bm{Q}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{T-L\times p^{2}L}
. Then,
\tilde{{\bm{V}}}=\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}=\tilde{{\bm{R}}}\tilde{{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{2}\tilde{{\bm{R}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}
. This further implies,
\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{1/2}=\tilde{{\bm{R}}}\tilde{{\bm{\Sigma}}}\tilde{{\bm{R}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}
, and
(\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{1/2})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}=\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{1/2}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\hat{{\bm{G}}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}-{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{G}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}

\displaystyle=(\textup{{vec}}(\hat{{\bm{G}}})-\textup{{vec}}({\bm{G}}))^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1}),

\displaystyle=(\textup{{vec}}(\hat{{\bm{G}}})-\textup{{vec}}({\bm{G}}))^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{1/2}\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{-1/2}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1}),

\displaystyle\leq\|{\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{1/2}(\textup{{vec}}(\hat{{\bm{G}}})-\textup{{vec}}({\bm{G}}))}\|_{\ell_{2}}\|{\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{-1/2}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1})}\|_{\ell_{2}},

\displaystyle\implies({\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\hat{{\bm{G}}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}-{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{G}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\textup{{vec}}(\hat{{\bm{G}}})-\textup{{vec}}({\bm{G}})}\|_{\tilde{{\bm{V}}}}^{2}\|{\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1}}\|_{\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{-1}}^{2}.

(6.14)

With this, we upper bound the expected output prediction error as follows,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[(\hat{y}_{T+1}-y_{T+1})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\big({\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\hat{{\bm{G}}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}-{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{G}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}-{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{F}}\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{T}-{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{e}}_{T}-z_{T+1}\big)^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}],

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{\leq}}2\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\hat{{\bm{G}}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}-{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{G}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}]+2\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{e}}_{T})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}]

\displaystyle+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{F}}\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{T})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}]+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[(z_{T+1})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}],

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{\leq}}2\|{\textup{{vec}}(\hat{{\bm{G}}})-\textup{{vec}}({\bm{G}})}\|_{\tilde{{\bm{V}}}}^{2}\|{\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1}}\|_{\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{-1}}^{2}+2{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{e}}_{T}{\bm{e}}_{T}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T}}]{\bm{u}}_{T+1}

\displaystyle+\|{{\bm{\Sigma}}_{w}}\|\|{{\bm{F}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{u}}_{T+1}}\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}+\sigma_{z}^{2},

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(c)}}}{{\leq}}2\|{\textup{{vec}}(\hat{{\bm{G}}})-\textup{{vec}}({\bm{G}})}\|_{\tilde{{\bm{V}}}}^{2}\|{\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1}}\|_{\tilde{{\bm{V}}}^{-1}}^{2}+2\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{C}}{\bm{A}}^{L}}\|^{2}\|{{\bm{\Gamma}}_{u}^{(T)}+{\bm{\Gamma}}_{w}^{(T)}}\|

\displaystyle+\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{\Sigma}}_{w}}\|\|{{\bm{F}}}\|_{F}^{2}+\sigma_{z}^{2},

(6.15)

where we obtain (a) from the pair-wise conditional independence of
\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{T},z_{T+1}
and the remaining terms, and using the identity
-2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
for any scalars
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
; (b) from (
6.14
); and (c) from upper bounding
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{e}}_{T}{\bm{e}}_{T}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T}}]
as follows,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{e}}_{T}{\bm{e}}_{T}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T}}]={\bm{C}}{\bm{A}}^{L}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{x}}_{T-L}{\bm{x}}_{T-L}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:T}]({\bm{A}}^{L})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{C}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}

\displaystyle={\bm{C}}{\bm{A}}^{L}\big({\bm{\Gamma}}_{u}^{(T)}+{\bm{\Gamma}}_{w}^{(T)}\big)({\bm{A}}^{L})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{C}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}},

\displaystyle\preceq\|{{\bm{C}}{\bm{A}}^{L}}\|^{2}\|{{\bm{\Gamma}}_{u}^{(T)}+{\bm{\Gamma}}_{w}^{(T)}}\|{\bm{I}}_{p},

(6.16)

where, we upper bound
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{x}}_{T-L}{\bm{x}}_{T-L}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:T}]
as follows,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{x}}_{T-L}{\bm{x}}_{T-L}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:T}]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\big(\sum_{i=0}^{T-L-1}{\bm{A}}^{i}{\bm{B}}{\bm{u}}_{T-L-i-1}+{\bm{A}}^{i}{\bm{w}}_{T-L-i-1}\big)\big(\sum_{i=0}^{T-L-1}{\bm{A}}^{i}{\bm{B}}{\bm{u}}_{T-L-i-1}+{\bm{A}}^{i}{\bm{w}}_{T-L-i-1}\big)^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:T}\big],

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{T-L-1}\sum_{j=0}^{T-L-1}{\bm{A}}^{i}{\bm{B}}{\bm{u}}_{T-L-i-1}{\bm{u}}_{T-L-j-1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{B}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}({\bm{A}}^{j})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}+\sum_{i=0}^{T-L-1}{\bm{A}}^{i}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{w}({\bm{A}}^{i})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}},

\displaystyle\preceq\underbrace{\sum_{i=0}^{T}\sum_{j=0}^{T}{\bm{A}}^{i}{\bm{B}}{\bm{u}}_{T-i}{\bm{u}}_{T-j}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{B}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}({\bm{A}}^{j})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}_{{\bm{\Gamma}}_{u}^{(T)}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=0}^{T}{\bm{A}}^{i}{\bm{\Sigma}}_{w}({\bm{A}}^{i})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}_{{\bm{\Gamma}}_{w}^{(T)}}.

(6.17)

Alternately, we can also upper bound the output prediction error in terms of the Frobenius norm of the estimation error. For this, we first note that,

\displaystyle({\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\hat{{\bm{G}}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}-{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{G}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T})^{2}\leq\|{\textup{{vec}}(\hat{{\bm{G}}})-\textup{{vec}}({\bm{G}})}\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\|{\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1}}\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\leq\beta^{4}L\|{\hat{{\bm{G}}}-{\bm{G}}}\|_{F},

(6.18)

where we use
\|{\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1}}\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}=(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1})=\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{u}}_{T+1}\leq\beta^{4}L
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[(\hat{y}_{T+1}-y_{T+1})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\hat{{\bm{G}}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T}-{\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{G}}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}]+2\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{e}}_{T})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}]

\displaystyle+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{u}}_{T+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{F}}\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{T})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}]+\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[(z_{T+1})^{2}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:{T+1}}],

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta^{4}L\|{\hat{{\bm{G}}}-{\bm{G}}}\|_{F}^{2}+2\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{C}}{\bm{A}}^{L}}\|^{2}\|{{\bm{\Gamma}}_{u}^{(T)}+{\bm{\Gamma}}_{w}^{(T)}}\|+\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{\Sigma}}_{w}}\|\|{{\bm{F}}}\|_{F}^{2}+\sigma_{z}^{2}.

(6.19)

This completes the proof.

6.2
Persistence of Excitation (Data-Independent Bounds)

In this section, we provide a lower bound on the smallest singular value of the design matrix. When combined with Theorem
3.1
, this gives consistent estimation of Markov parameter matrix
{\bm{G}}
. We will guarantee persistence of excitation under two different assumptions. Our first result provides a two-sided concentration bound under the assumption of bounded inputs with zero-mean and isotropic covariance, whereas, our second result provides a one-sided concentration bound under very mild assumption on the inputs, that is, bounded second and fourth moments.

6.2.1
Two-sided Concentration Bound – entire spectrum

Before we state our result, recall Assumption
3
(
{\bm{a}}
) from Section
3
. For the sake of convenience, we are repeating the statement of Assumption
3
(
{\bm{a}}
) here.

Assumption
3
(
{\bm{a}}
)

(Bounded inputs)
\{{\bm{u}}_{t}\}_{t=0}^{T}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{D}_{u}
are stochastic with zero mean
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{u}}_{t}]=0
, isotropic covariance
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{u}}_{t}{\bm{u}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}]={\bm{I}}_{p}
and have bounded Euclidean norm, i.e., there exists a scalar
\beta>0
such that
\|{{\bm{u}}_{t}}\|_{\ell_{2}}\leq\beta
for all
t\in[T]
.

We remark that the assumption of bounded inputs can be relaxed to include unbounded stochastic inputs as well. For example, in the case of sub-Gaussian inputs, it is easy to show that, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the inputs are bounded as
\|{{\bm{u}}_{t}}\|_{\ell_{2}}\lesssim\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{p\log(T/\delta)})
for all
t\in[T]
. Similarly, the assumption of isotropic inputs can also be relaxed to input with positive-definite covariance
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{u}}_{t}{\bm{u}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}]\succ 0
. Using Assumption
(

a

)
, we get the following persistence of excitation result.

Proposition 6.4
(Persistence of Excitation)

Consider a sequence of inputs
\{{\bm{u}}_{t}\}_{t=0}^{T}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{D}_{u}
satisfying Assumptions
(

a

)
, and let
\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}:=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{u}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}&{\bm{u}}_{t-1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}&\cdots&{\bm{u}}_{t-L+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\end{bmatrix}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}
. Then, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda_{\min}\left(\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})^{\top}\right)\geq(1-\varepsilon)^{2}(T-L)\right)\geq 1-9^{p^{2}L}\cdot 2(L+1)\exp\left(-\frac{(T-L)\varepsilon^{2}}{2(L+1)L\beta^{4}}\right).

Remark 6.3

Note that we can also re-write the statement of Proposition
6.4
as follows: for all
\delta\in(0,1)
, for all
\varepsilon>0
, the following event

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})^{\top}\right)\geq(1-\varepsilon)^{2}(T-L)

(6.20)

holds with probability at least
1-\delta
, provided that

\displaystyle T-L\geq\frac{2(L+1)L\beta^{4}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\left(\log\left(\frac{2(L+1)}{\delta}\right)+p^{2}L\log(9)\right).

(6.21)

Recalling (
3.2
), this is equivalent to
\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})\geq(1-\varepsilon)^{2}(T-L)
, which shows persistence of excitation.

Proof
For ease of notation, we introduce
\tilde{{\bm{U}}}=\begin{bmatrix}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{L}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{L+1}&\ldots&\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{T-1}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{T}\end{bmatrix}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}
and note that,

\displaystyle\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}=\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})^{\top}.

(6.22)

By further noting that
(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})^{\top}=(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}^{\top})\otimes({\bm{u}}_{t+1}{\bm{u}}_{t+1}^{\top})
, and that
\{{\bm{u}}_{t}\}_{t=0}^{T}
are isotropic due to Assumption
(

a

)
, we can also write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}]=\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}[(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})^{\top}]=(T-L){\bm{I}}_{p^{2}L}.

(6.23)

Our proof proceeds by establishing a concentration bound on
\|\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}]\|
. We note, by the variational form of the operator norm, that

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}]\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}-(T-L){\bm{I}}_{p^{2}L}\right\|=\sup_{{\bm{v}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}}\left|\|\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}\|^{2}_{2}-(T-L)\right|^{2}.

(6.24)

The rest of the proof proceeds as follows:

\bullet

Step 1) Blocking:
Letting
{\bm{v}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\|\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}\|^{2}_{2}-(T-L)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}|(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})^{\top}{\bm{v}}|^{2}-(T-L)\right|,

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i=0}^{L}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{(T-L)/(L+1)}|(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{k(L+1)+i-1}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{k(L+1)+i})^{\top}{\bm{v}}|^{2}-\frac{T-L}{L+1}\right)\right|,

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{L}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{(T-L)/(L+1)}\left(|(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{k(L+1)+i-1}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{k(L+1)+i})^{\top}{\bm{v}}|^{2}-1\right)\right|,

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{L}S_{i}.

(6.25)

where we define
S_{i}=\left|\sum_{k=1}^{(T-L)/(L+1)}\left(|(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{k(L+1)+i-1}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{k(L+1)+i})^{\top}{\bm{v}}|^{2}-1\right)\right|
for all
i\in\{0,\dots,L\}
, and make the simplifying assumption that
T-L
can be divided by
L+1
. (
Note that, this goes without loss of generality, and we assume it for the sake of clarity. It can be easily avoided by noting that

\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})^{\top}\succeq\sum_{t=L}^{(L+1)\lfloor\frac{T-L}{L+1}\rfloor+L}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})^{\top},

where
\lfloor\cdot\rfloor
denotes the floor operator. We can then analyze everything with
T_{0}=(L+1)\lfloor\frac{T-L}{L+1}\rfloor+L
, and note that
T-L\leq T_{0}\leq T-1
.
)

\bullet

Step 2) Hoeffding’s inequality:
Next, let
i\in\{0,\dots,L\}
, and let us provide a concentration bound on
S_{i}
. Recall that for all
t\in[T]
,
\|{{\bm{u}}_{t}}\|_{\ell_{2}}\leq\beta
due to Assumption
(

a

)
, and
\|{{\bm{v}}}\|_{\ell_{2}}=1
, thus, we can immediately verify that for all
k\in[(T-L)/(L+1)]
,
0\leq|(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{k(L+1)+i-1}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{k(L+1)+i})^{\top}{\bm{v}}|^{2}\leq L\beta^{4}
. Moreover, we note that
((\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{k(L+1)+i-1}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{k(L+1)+i})^{\top}{\bm{v}})_{k\geq 1}
are independent, thus Hoeffding’s inequality applies and we obtain: for all
\rho>0
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(S_{i}\geq\rho\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{2(L+1)\rho^{2}}{(T-L)L\beta^{4}}\right).

(6.26)

Observe that the event
\sum_{i=0}^{L}S_{i}>\rho
is included in the event that there exists
i\in\{0,\dots,L\}
,
S_{i}>\rho/(L+1)
. Therefore, we obtain by union bound that for all
\rho\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{L}S_{i}>\rho\right)\leq\sum_{i=0}^{L}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(S_{i}>\frac{\rho}{L+1}\right)\leq 2(L+1)\exp\left(-\frac{2\rho^{2}}{(T-L)(L+1)L\beta^{4}}\right).

(6.27)

Hence, we have just established that for all
{\bm{v}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}
, for all
\rho>0
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\|\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}\|^{2}_{2}-(T-L)\right|>\rho\right)\leq 2(L+1)\exp\left(-\frac{2\rho^{2}}{(T-L)(L+1)L\beta^{4}}\right).

\bullet

Step 3) Covering with
1/4
-net:
Next, we may use a
\epsilon
-net argument to conclude a bound on
\|\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}]\|
. Indeed, by using Lemma 2.5 in
[
54
]
with
\epsilon=1/4
, we obtain that for all
\rho>0
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}]\|>\rho\right)

\displaystyle\leq 9^{p^{2}L}\max_{{\bm{v}}\in\mathcal{N}_{1/4}}\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\|\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}\|^{2}_{2}-(T-L)\right|>\rho/2\right),

\displaystyle\leq 9^{p^{2}L}\cdot 2(L+1)\exp\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}}{2(T-L)(L+1)L\beta^{4}}\right),

(6.28)

where
\mathcal{N}_{1/4}
is a minimal
1/4
-net of the unit sphere
\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}
. Reparameterizing
\rho=(T-L)\varepsilon
and using Lemma 3 in
[
55
]
, we obtain that for all
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sigma_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}})\geq(1-\varepsilon)\sqrt{T-L}\right)\geq 1-9^{p^{2}L}\cdot 2(L+1)\exp\left(-\frac{(T-L)\varepsilon^{2}}{2(L+1)L\beta^{4}}\right).

Recalling that
\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})=\sigma_{\min}^{2}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}})
concludes the proof.

6.3.1
One-sided Concentration Bound – lower tail of the spectrum

In this section, we present an alternate persistence of excitation guarantee under very mild assumptions using the proof techniques adapted from
[
28
]
. Specifically, we work with stochastic inputs
\{{\bm{u}}_{t}\}_{t=0}^{T}
that satisfy following properties.

Assumption
3
(
{\bm{b}}
)

(Heavy-tailed inputs)
\{{\bm{u}}_{t}\}_{t=0}^{T}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{D}_{u}
are stochastic with zero mean
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{u}}_{t}]=0
, isotropic covariance
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{u}}_{t}{\bm{u}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}]={\bm{I}}_{p}
and there exists a scalar
m_{4}>0
such that
\sup_{{\bm{v}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1}))^{4}]\leq m_{4}
for all
t\in[T]
, where
\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}:=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{u}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}&{\bm{u}}_{t-1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}&\cdots&{\bm{u}}_{t-L+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\end{bmatrix}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}
.

Assumption
(

b

)
can be satisfied by many distributions
\mathcal{D}_{u}
. In Section
A.4
, we show that in the case of Gaussian inputs, Assumption
(

b

)
holds with
m_{4}=9
. We remark that the assumption of isotropic inputs is for the clarity of exposition, and can be relaxed to input with positive-definite covariance
\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{u}}_{t}{\bm{u}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}]\succ 0
. Using Assumption
(

b

)
, we get the following lower bound on the smallest eigenvalue of the (scaled) empirical covariance matrix.

Proposition 6.5
(Persistence of Excitation)

Consider a sequence of inputs
\{{\bm{u}}_{t}\}_{t=0}^{T}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{D}_{u}
satisfying Assumptions
(

b

)
, and let
\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}:=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{u}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}&{\bm{u}}_{t-1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}&\cdots&{\bm{u}}_{t-L+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\end{bmatrix}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}
. Suppose the sequence length satisfies the following lower bound,

\displaystyle T-L

\displaystyle\geq 32(L+1)m_{4}\left(\log\left(\frac{2(L+1)}{\delta}\right)+p^{2}L\log\left(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta}\right)\right)

(6.29)

Then, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\lambda_{\min}\left(\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})^{\top}\right)\geq(T-L)/4\right)\geq 1-\delta.

Proof

To begin, recall that

\displaystyle\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}=\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1})(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}})=\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1}{\bm{u}}_{t+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}=:\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}.

(6.30)

Then, using Assumption
(

b

)
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}]\otimes\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{u}}_{t+1}{\bm{u}}_{t+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}]=\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}{\bm{I}}_{pL}\otimes{\bm{I}}_{p}=(T-L){\bm{I}}_{p^{2}L}.

(6.31)

Next, letting
{\bm{v}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}
, consider the quantity,

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}=\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t})^{2},

(6.32)

and note that the above quantity can be viewed as a summation of the random process
\{({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t})^{2}\}_{t=L}^{T-1}
. In the following we will derive a one-sided concentration bound for this random process. The rest of the proof proceeds as follows:

\bullet

Step 1) Blocking:
We begin by using blocking technique, similar to the proof of Proposition
6.4
, to get independent samples, as follows,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t})^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{L}\sum_{\tau=1}^{(T-L)/(L+1)}({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau(L+1)+k-1})^{2},

(6.33)

where we make the simplifying assumption that
T-L
can be divided by
L+1
. Recall that, this can be relaxed as discussed in the proof of Proposition
6.4
.

\bullet

Step 2) Bernstein’s inequality for non-negative random variables:
Before we apply Bernstein’s inequality, we upper bound the mean and second moment of the random variable
({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t})^{2}
using Assumption
(

b

)
as follows,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[{\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{v}}]={\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}]{\bm{v}}={\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{I}}_{p^{2}L}{\bm{v}}=1.

(6.34)

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t})^{4}]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}(\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{t}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{t+1}))^{4}]\leq m_{4}.

(6.35)

Then, using one-sided Bernstein’s inequality for non-negative random variables, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sum_{\tau=1}^{(T-L)/(L+1)}\big(({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau(L+1)+k-1})^{2}-\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau(L+1)+k-1})^{2}]\big)\leq-\frac{T-L}{(L+1)}z\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}\bigg),

(6.36)

\displaystyle\implies\quad\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sum_{\tau=1}^{(T-L)/(L+1)}({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau(L+1)+k-1})^{2}\leq\frac{T-L}{L}(1-z)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}\bigg).

(6.37)

Union bounding
L+1
such events, we obtain,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{L}\sum_{\tau=1}^{(T-L)/(L+1)}({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau(L+1)+k-1})^{2}\leq(T-L)(1-z)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq(L+1)\exp\bigg(-\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}\bigg).

(6.38)

This can be alternately represented by letting

\displaystyle(L+1)\exp\bigg(-\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\delta,

\displaystyle\iff\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}

\displaystyle=\log((L+1)/\delta),

\displaystyle\impliedby z

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{L+1}{T-L}2m_{4}\log((L+1)/\delta)}.

(6.39)

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sum_{t=L}^{T-1}({\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{u}}}_{t})^{2}\leq(T-L)-\sqrt{2m_{4}(L+1)(T-L)\log((L+1)/\delta)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\delta.

(6.40)

\bullet

Step 3) Covering with
{\delta}/{(8p^{2}L)}
-net:
Next, we will use a covering argument to lower bound the minimum eigenvalue of the (scaled) empirical covariance matrix as follows: Let
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}:=\{{\bm{v}}_{1},{\bm{v}}_{2},\dots,{\bm{v}}_{|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|}\}\subset\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}
be the
\epsilon
-net of
\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}
such that for any
{\bm{v}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}
, there exists
{\bm{v}}_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
such that
\|{{\bm{v}}-{\bm{v}}_{i}}\|_{\ell_{2}}\leq\epsilon
. From Lemma 5.2 of
[
56
]
, we have
|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\leq(1+2/\epsilon)^{p^{2}L}
.

Let us choose
{\bm{v}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p^{2}L-1}
for which
\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})={\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}
, and choose
{\bm{v}}_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
which approximates
{\bm{v}}
as
\|{{\bm{v}}-{\bm{v}}_{i}}\|_{\ell_{2}}\leq\epsilon
. By triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle|{\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}-{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}|

\displaystyle=|{\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}({\bm{v}}-{\bm{v}}_{i})+({\bm{v}}-{\bm{v}}_{i})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}|,

\displaystyle\leq\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|\|{{\bm{v}}}\|_{\ell_{2}}\|{{\bm{v}}-{\bm{v}}_{i}}\|_{\ell_{2}}+\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|\|{{\bm{v}}_{i}}\|_{\ell_{2}}\|{{\bm{v}}-{\bm{v}}_{i}}\|_{\ell_{2}},

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|.

(6.41)

This further implies,

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}\geq{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}-2\epsilon\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|,

\displaystyle\implies\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})\geq\inf_{{\bm{v}}_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}-2\epsilon\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|.

(6.42)

Hence, in order to lower bound
\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})
, we also need an upper bound on
\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|
. This can be done as follows: First, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|]=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\lambda_{\max}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})]\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\textup{{tr}}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})]=\textup{{tr}}\big(\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}]\big)=(T-L)\textup{{tr}}\big({\bm{I}}_{p^{2}L}\big)=p^{2}L(T-L).

(6.43)

Then, using Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|>\frac{p^{2}L(T-L)}{\delta}\bigg)\leq\frac{\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}[\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|]}{p^{2}L(T-L)}\delta\leq\delta.

(6.44)

Let
\mathcal{E}:=\{\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|\leq\frac{2p^{2}L(T-L)}{\delta}\}
denote the event that
\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|
is bounded by the specified threshold. Then, it is straightforward to see that
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta/2
. This also implies,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})<(T-L)(1/2-z)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\big\{\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})<(T-L)(1/2-z)\big\}\bigcap\mathcal{E}\bigg)+\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}^{c}),

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\big\{\inf_{{\bm{v}}_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}-2\epsilon\|{\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}}\|<(T-L)(1/2-z)\big\}\bigcap\mathcal{E}\bigg)+\delta/2,

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\inf_{{\bm{v}}_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}<(T-L)(1/2-z)+4\epsilon\frac{p^{2}L(T-L)}{\delta}\bigg)+\delta/2,

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\inf_{{\bm{v}}_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}<(T-L)(1/2-z+\frac{4\epsilon p^{2}L}{\delta})\bigg)+\delta/2,

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\inf_{{\bm{v}}_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}<(T-L)(1-z)\bigg)+\delta/2,

(6.45)

where we obtained the last inequality by choosing
\epsilon=\frac{\delta}{8p^{2}L}
. Using (
6.38
) with union bounding over all the elements in
\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}
, we obtain,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\inf_{{\bm{v}}_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}<(T-L)(1-z)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|(L+1)\exp\bigg(-\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}\bigg).

(6.46)

From Lemma 5.2 of
[
56
]
, we have
|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\leq(1+2/\epsilon)^{p^{2}L}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\inf_{{\bm{v}}_{i}\in\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}}{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}}{\bm{v}}_{i}<(T-L)(1-z)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta})^{p^{2}L}\cdot(L+1)\exp\bigg(-\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}\bigg).

(6.47)

This can be alternately represented by letting,

\displaystyle(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta})^{p^{2}L}\cdot(L+1)\exp\bigg(-\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\delta/2,

\displaystyle\iff\exp\bigg(-\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\delta/(2(L+1))\cdot(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta})^{-p^{2}L},

\displaystyle\iff\frac{(T-L)z^{2}}{2(L+1)m_{4}}

\displaystyle=\log(2(L+1)/\delta)+p^{2}L\log\big(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta}\big),

\displaystyle\impliedby z

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{\frac{2(L+1)m_{4}}{(T-L)}\big(\log\big(\frac{2(L+1)}{\delta}\big)+p^{2}L\log\big(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta}\big)\big)}$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.67776pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}.

(6.48)

Plugging this back into (
6.45
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})<(T-L)/2-\sqrt{2(L+1)(T-L)m_{4}\big(\log\big(\frac{2(L+1)}{\delta}\big)+p^{2}L\log\big(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta}\big)\big)}\bigg)\leq\delta

(6.49)

Finally, choosing the trajectory length via

\displaystyle(T-L)/4

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{2(L+1)(T-L)m_{4}\big(\log\big(\frac{2(L+1)}{\delta}\big)+p^{2}L\log\big(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta}\big)\big)},

\displaystyle\iff(T-L)/16

\displaystyle\geq 2(L+1)m_{4}\big(\log\big(\frac{2(L+1)}{\delta}\big)+p^{2}L\log\big(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta}\big)\big),

\displaystyle\impliedby T-L

\displaystyle\geq 32(L+1)m_{4}\big(\log\big(\frac{2(L+1)}{\delta}\big)+p^{2}L\log\big(1+\frac{16p^{2}L}{\delta}\big)\big),

(6.50)

we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\big(\lambda_{\min}(\tilde{{\bm{U}}}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\tilde{{\bm{U}}})\geq(T-L)/4\big)\geq 1-\delta.

(6.51)

This completes the proof.

6.3.2
Proof of Theorem
3.4

Proof

The proof of Theorem
3.4
relies on combining The proofs of Theorems
3.1
and
3.3
. We begin by defining the event
\mathcal{E}:=\{\lambda_{\min}\big(\sum_{\tau=L}^{T-1}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\big)\geq(T-L)/4\}
. Then, under the conditions of Theorem
3.3
, we have
\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta
. Next, recalling the definition of
\Xi
from (
6.8
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|{\hat{{\bm{G}}}-{\bm{G}}}\|_{F}>\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{\frac{4p^{2}L\Xi}{\delta(T-L)}}$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.67776pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}+2\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{B}}}\|\|{{\bm{C}}}\|\frac{\phi({\bm{A}},\rho)\rho^{L}}{1-\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\|{\hat{{\bm{G}}}-{\bm{G}}}\|_{F}>\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{\frac{4p^{2}L\Xi}{\delta(T-L)}}$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.67776pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}+2\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{B}}}\|\|{{\bm{C}}}\|\frac{\phi({\bm{A}},\rho)\rho^{L}}{1-\rho}\right\}\bigcap\mathcal{E}\right)+\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}^{c}),

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\operatorname{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\|{\hat{{\bm{G}}}-{\bm{G}}}\|_{F}\leq\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{\frac{p^{2}L\Xi}{\delta\lambda_{\min}\big(\sum_{\tau=L}^{T-1}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\big)}}$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.67776pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}+\frac{\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{B}}}\|\|{{\bm{C}}}\|\frac{\phi({\bm{A}},\rho)\rho^{L}}{1-\rho}\sqrt{T-L}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}\big(\sum_{\tau=L}^{T-1}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}\bar{{\bm{u}}}_{\tau}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\big)}}\bigg)+\delta,

\displaystyle\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{\leq}}2\delta,

(6.52)

where we obtain (a) from (
6.13
) in Section
6.1.1
. Hence, under the conditions of Theorems
3.1
and
3.3
, with probability at least
1-2\delta
, we have

\|{\hat{{\bm{G}}}-{\bm{G}}}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{9p^{2}L\Xi}/\sqrt{\delta(T-L)},

provided that we choose
L
via,

\displaystyle 2\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{B}}}\|\|{{\bm{C}}}\|\frac{\phi({\bm{A}},\rho)\rho^{L}}{1-\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{\frac{p^{2}L\Xi}{\delta(T-L)}}$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.67776pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt},

\displaystyle\iff\rho^{L}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-\rho)}{2\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{B}}}\|\|{{\bm{C}}}\|\phi({\bm{A}},\rho)}\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqrt{}$}}}}}\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 2.5pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\overline{\frac{p^{2}L\Xi}{\delta(T-L)}}$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.67776pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt}}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\hbox{}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 2.5pt},

\displaystyle\impliedby L

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2\log(\rho^{-1})}\log\left(\frac{(T-L)\delta}{p^{2}L\Xi}\right)+\frac{1}{\log(\rho^{-1})}\log\left(\frac{2\beta^{2}\|{{\bm{B}}}\|\|{{\bm{C}}}\|\phi({\bm{A}},\rho)}{1-\rho}\right).

(6.53)

finally replacing
\delta
with
\delta/2
gives the statement of the theorem.

6.4
State-space Parameter Estimation

6.4.1
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Proof

The proof is similar to that in
[
44
]
, except that it is slightly modified to get the upper bounds in terms of
\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}
instead of
\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|
. Recalling Algorithm 1 in
[
22
]
, let

•

{\bm{H}},{\bm{H}}^{-},{\bm{H}}^{+},{\bm{L}},{\bm{O}},{\bm{Q}}
be the matrices corresponding to
{\bm{G}}
.

•

\hat{{\bm{H}}},\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-},\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{+},\hat{{\bm{L}}},\hat{{\bm{O}}},\hat{{\bm{Q}}}
be the matrices corresponding to
\hat{{\bm{G}}}
.

•

\bar{{\bm{A}}},\bar{{\bm{B}}},\bar{{\bm{C}}}
be the output of Ho-Kalman Algorithm with input
{\bm{H}}
.

•

\hat{{\bm{A}}},\hat{{\bm{B}}},\hat{{\bm{C}}}
be the output of Ho-Kalman Algorithm with input
\hat{{\bm{H}}}
.

\bullet

Step (1)

Perturbation bounds for
{\bm{H}},\hat{{\bm{H}}}
and
{\bm{L}},\hat{{\bm{L}}}
:

Let
{\bm{H}}[i]-\hat{{\bm{H}}}[i]\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p(L/2+1)}
denote the
i
-th block row of
{\bm{H}}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}
. Since each
{\bm{H}}[i]-\hat{{\bm{H}}}[i]
is a sub-matrix of the Markov parameters matrix difference
{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}
, we have
\|{{\bm{H}}[i]-\hat{{\bm{H}}}[i]}\|_{F}\leq\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}
. This further implies,

\displaystyle\|{{\bm{H}}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{L/2}\|{{\bm{H}}[i]-\hat{{\bm{H}}}[i]}\|_{F}^{2}\leq L/2\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}^{2}.

(6.54)

Since
{\bm{H}}^{-}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-}
and
{\bm{H}}^{+}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{+}
are the sub-matrices of
{\bm{H}}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}
, therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|{{\bm{H}}^{-}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|{{\bm{H}}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{L/2}\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}.

(6.55)

\displaystyle\|{{\bm{H}}^{+}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{+}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|{{\bm{H}}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{L/2}\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}.

(6.56)

To proceed, recall that
{\bm{L}}={\bm{H}}^{-}
by definition and
\hat{{\bm{L}}}
is the best rank-
n
approximations of
\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-}
. This implies that
\|{\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-}-\hat{{\bm{L}}}}\|_{F}\leq\|{\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-}-{\bm{M}}}\|_{F}
, for any rank-
n
matrix
{\bm{M}}
. Choose
{\bm{M}}={\bm{H}}^{-}
, we have

\displaystyle\|{{\bm{L}}-\hat{{\bm{L}}}}\|_{F}\leq\|{{\bm{L}}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-}}\|_{F}+\|{\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-}-\hat{{\bm{L}}}}\|_{F}\leq\|{{\bm{H}}^{-}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-}}\|_{F}+\|{\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{-}-{\bm{H}}^{-}}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{2L}\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}.

(6.57)

\bullet

Step (2)

Perturbation bounds for
\bar{{\bm{O}}},\hat{{\bm{O}}}
and
\bar{{\bm{Q}}},\hat{{\bm{Q}}}
:
Let
{\bm{L}},\hat{{\bm{L}}}
have the singular value decomposition
{\bm{L}}={\bm{U}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{V}}^{*}
, and
\hat{{\bm{L}}}=\hat{{\bm{U}}}\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}\hat{{\bm{V}}}^{*}
. Then. from Algorithm 1 in
[
44
]
, we have
\bar{{\bm{O}}}={\bm{U}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}
,
\bar{{\bm{Q}}}={\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}{\bm{V}}^{*}
,
\hat{{\bm{O}}}=\hat{{\bm{U}}}\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}
, and
\hat{{\bm{Q}}}=\hat{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\hat{{\bm{V}}}^{*}
. Let
{\bm{T}}
be an
n\times n
unitary matrix. Then, applying Lemma 5.14 of
[
57
]
with the robustness condition
\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})\geq 2\|{{\bm{L}}-\hat{{\bm{L}}}}\|
, we get

\displaystyle\|{\bar{{\bm{O}}}-\hat{{\bm{O}}}{\bm{T}}}\|_{F}^{2}+\|{\bar{{\bm{Q}}}-{\bm{T}}^{-1}\hat{{\bm{Q}}}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{2}-1}\frac{\|{{\bm{L}}-\hat{{\bm{L}}}}\|_{F}^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}.

(6.58)

Combining this with (
6.57
), we have

\displaystyle\|{\bar{{\bm{O}}}-\hat{{\bm{O}}}{\bm{T}}}\|_{F}^{2}+\|{\bar{{\bm{Q}}}-{\bm{T}}^{*}\hat{{\bm{Q}}}}\|_{F}^{2}\leq 10L\frac{\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}.

(6.59)

\bullet

Step (3)

Perturbation bounds for
\bar{{\bm{B}}},\hat{{\bm{B}}}
, and
\bar{{\bm{C}}},\hat{{\bm{C}}}
:

Since
\bar{{\bm{C}}}-\hat{{\bm{C}}}{\bm{T}}
is a sub-matrix of
\bar{{\bm{O}}}-\hat{{\bm{O}}}{\bm{T}}
, and
{\bm{B}}-{\bm{T}}^{*}{\bm{B}}
is a sub-matrix of
\bar{{\bm{Q}}}-{\bm{T}}^{*}\hat{{\bm{Q}}}
, it immediately follows that,

\displaystyle\|{\bar{{\bm{C}}}-\hat{{\bm{C}}}{\bm{T}}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bar{{\bm{O}}}-\hat{{\bm{O}}}{\bm{T}}}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{\frac{10L}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}}\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F},

(6.60)

\displaystyle\|{\bar{{\bm{B}}}-{\bm{T}}^{*}\hat{{\bm{B}}}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bar{{\bm{Q}}}-{\bm{T}}^{*}\hat{{\bm{Q}}}}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{\frac{10L}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}}\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}.

(6.61)

\bullet

Step (4)

Perturbation bounds for
\bar{{\bm{A}}},\hat{{\bm{A}}}
:
Let
{\bm{X}}=\hat{{\bm{O}}}{\bm{T}}
, and
{\bm{Y}}={\bm{T}}^{*}\hat{{\bm{Q}}}
. Then, from equation (30-31) in
[
44
]
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\bar{{\bm{A}}}-{\bm{T}}^{*}\hat{{\bm{A}}}{\bm{T}}}\|_{F}\leq\|{(\bar{{\bm{O}}}^{\dagger}-{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}){\bm{H}}^{+}\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}}\|_{F}+\|{{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}({\bm{H}}^{+}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{+})\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}}\|_{F}+\|{{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{+}(\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}-{\bm{Y}}^{\dagger})}\|_{F}.

(6.62)

From Appendix F in
[
44
]
, we have

\displaystyle\|{(\bar{{\bm{O}}}^{\dagger}-{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}){\bm{H}}^{+}\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bar{{\bm{O}}}^{\dagger}-{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}}\|_{F}\|{{\bm{H}}^{+}}\|\|{\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}}\|\leq\|{\bar{{\bm{O}}}-{\bm{X}}}\|_{F}\max\{\|{{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}}\|^{2},\|{\bar{{\bm{O}}}^{\dagger}}\|^{2}\}\|{{\bm{H}}^{+}}\|\|{\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}}\|,

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{10L}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}}\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}\big(\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}\big)\big(\sqrt{\frac{2}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}}\big)\|{{\bm{H}}^{+}}\|,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{9\sqrt{L}\|{{\bm{H}}}\|}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})^{2}}\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}.

(6.63)

Next, from Appendix F in
[
44
]
, we also have

\displaystyle\|{{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}({\bm{H}}^{+}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{+})\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|{{\bm{H}}^{+}-\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{+}}\|_{F}\|{{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}}\|\|{\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2L}}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}.

(6.64)

\displaystyle\text{and,}\;\;\|{{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{+}(\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}-{\bm{Y}}^{\dagger})}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bar{{\bm{Q}}}^{\dagger}-{\bm{Y}}^{\dagger}}\|_{F}\|{\hat{{\bm{H}}}^{+}}\|\|{{\bm{X}}^{\dagger}}\|\leq\frac{9\sqrt{L}\|{\hat{{\bm{H}}}}\|}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})^{2}}\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}.

(6.65)

Combining (
6.63
), (
6.64
), and (
6.65
) into (
6.62
), and using Appendix G in
[
44
]
we have

\displaystyle\|{\bar{{\bm{A}}}-{\bm{T}}^{*}\hat{{\bm{A}}}{\bm{T}}}\|_{F}\leq\big(\frac{9\sqrt{L}(\|{{\bm{H}}}\|+\|{\hat{{\bm{H}}}}\|)}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{2L}}{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}\big)\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}

(6.66)

Observe that the robustness condition is satisfied by assuming
\|{{\bm{G}}-\hat{{\bm{G}}}}\|_{F}\leq\frac{\sigma_{\min}({\bm{L}})}{2\sqrt{2L}}

7
Conclusion and Discussion

We provide the first non-asymptotic error bounds and sample complexity analysis for learning a realization of a partially observed dynamical system with linear state transitions and bilinear observations, given a single finite trajectory of input-output samples. Under stability and very mild assumptions on the process and measurement noises, we provide both data-dependent error bound, precisely capturing the shape of uncertainty, and data independent error bound, optimally scaling as
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(1/\sqrt{T-L})
. Our results show that despite getting heavy-tailed covariates and overall noise process due to bilinear observation, the non-asymptotic error bounds for bilinear-observation system are similar to that of the standard LTI system with partial state observation, except some additional dependencies arise because of using heavy-tailed tools. Of independent interest, we also establish persistence of excitation results for the heavy-tailed and correlated covariates used to learn the system’s Markov parameters. Finally, numerical experiments corroborate our theory and highlight a trade-off between the estimation error and the system’s memory.

There are several open questions leading to interesting directions for future work.
First, can our method be extended to linear/bilinear systems which are marginally stable, i.e. the spectral radius
\rho({\bm{A}})=1
?
Our current estimator (
3.1
) is biased by the state
L
steps in the past, which is not guaranteed to shrink if the dynamics are only marginally stable.
One possible approach for overcoming this challenge is to build an estimator that regresses the output against a finite history of inputs
and
outputs.
Such an estimator arises naturally from the predictor form of the Kalman filter, which has been used for identification in the LTI setting
[
23
,
16
]
.
In our linear/bilinear setting, the predictor form involves
bilinear
transitions,
so it is not straightforward to immediately extend this technique.
Alternatively, estimators can be constructed from instrumental variables in a two stage regression process. This perspective has been successfully used for LTI identification
[
24
,
29
]
, but its extension to the linear/bilinear setting is similarly nontrivial.
Lastly, spectral filtering is an alternative method for handling marginal stability in LTI systems, though it has been used mainly for prediction rather than identification
[
20
,
58
]
.

Second, what is the best way to make
predictions
in this linear/bilinear setting?
When the parameters
(A,B,C)
are known and the inputs are observed, filtering techniques for linear time-varying (LTV) systems are directly applicable.
It is of interest to characterize the sensitivity of these methods to estimation errors, or to develop robust methods, in order to analyze the sample complexity of state estimation and output prediction
when the dynamics are unknown.
Another interesting direction is to investigate filtering approaches that can use estimated Markov parameter directly, sidestepping the state-space recovery step
[
59
]
.
Lastly, since the observations are bilinearly influenced by inputs, it is interesting to consider how the choice of inputs can enhance (or diminish) achievable prediction accuracy.
This line of inquiry is related to classic problems in sensor design and placement
[
2
,
60
]
.

Finally, what is the best achievable
control performance
given limited data from a linear/bilinear system?
This is a rich but challenging question:
even if the dynamics are known, the optimal control problem does not have a straightforward tractable solution, even for simple linear or quadratic costs.
Due to the impact of inputs on observations,
the separation principle does not hold and there is a direct trade-off between inputs which are useful for estimating versus controlling the state.

Appendix A
Proofs of Supporting Results

A.1
Proof of Lemma
6.1

Proof

We begin our proof by first estimating the conditional mean of the effective noise process
\{\zeta_{\tau+1}\}_{\tau=L}^{T-1}
, as follows,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\zeta_{\tau+1}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:T}\big]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[\bar{{\bm{w}}}_{\tau}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\otimes{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\textup{{vec}}({\bm{F}})+{\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{e}}_{\tau+1}+z_{\tau+1}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:T}\big],

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[{\bm{e}}_{\tau+1}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:T}\big]={\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{C}}{\bm{A}}^{L}\operatorname{\mathbb{E}}\big[{\bm{x}}_{\tau-L+1}{~\big|~}{\bm{u}}_{1:T}\big],

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{C}}{\bm{A}}^{L}\sum_{i=0}^{\tau-L}{\bm{A}}^{\tau-L-i}{\bm{B}}{\bm{u}}_{i}={\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}{\bm{C}}{\bm{A}}^{L}\sum_{i=0}^{\tau-L}{\bm{A}}^{\tau-L-i}{\bm{B}}{\bm{u}}_{i},

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}_{\tau+1}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\sum_{i=0}^{\tau-L}{\bm{C}}{\bm{A}}^{\tau-i}{\bm{B}}{\bm{u}}_{i}.

(A.1)

where we used the fact that
